chromeos: Record shutdown trigger and workload metrics

On ChromeOS, the shutdown-type crash key only reflects whether
browsers were closeable when ExitIgnoreUnloadHandlers() ran, so it
cannot distinguish user logout, restart-to-apply-flags, external
SIGTERM from session_manager, and other exit paths. Record a dedicated
cros-shutdown-trigger crash key at the first shutdown entry point
(AttemptUserExit, AttemptRestartInternal, or
ExitHandler::ExitWhenPossibleOnUIThread, with a kOther fallback in
OnShutdownStarting).

Also record cros-shutdown-elapsed-ms with the cumulative milliseconds
elapsed at each SetShutdownPhase transition, and emit
Shutdown.ChromeOS.TabsAtShutdown,
Shutdown.ChromeOS.RenderersAtShutdown, and
Shutdown.ChromeOS.DBusShutdownDuration to attribute teardown cost.

TAG=agy
CONV=acea79f2-f3ae-48fd-a985-33bcb9d543e4

[gfx] Use InlinedVector for script extensions in RenderTextHarfBuzz

GetScriptExtensions() was returning a heap-allocated std::vector of
up to 32 UScriptCodes on every call in ScriptSetIntersect() and
ScriptInterval(). Using absl::InlinedVector<UScriptCode, 32> keeps
the script buffer on the stack and avoids heap allocation.

[PartitionAlloc] Re-enable DynamicSizeThresholdPurge

PartitionAllocThreadCacheTest.DynamicSizeThresholdPurge was disabled
in crrev.com/c/3395415 (crbug.com/1287811) due to flakiness.

The test verifies that lowering the dynamic size threshold traps
previously-cached large allocations and that Purge() successfully
reclaims them. Subsequent optimizations and fixes across
PartitionAlloc's thread cache have resolved the underlying instability.
Local verification across 50 iterations (100 test runs across both
AlternateBucketDistributionAndPartitionFreeList parameterizations)
passed with 0 failures or flakes.

This CL removes the DISABLED_ modifier and re-enables
DynamicSizeThresholdPurge.

Test: base_unittests --gtest_filter="*DynamicSizeThresholdPurge*"

Lens: Own LensOverlayControllerAndroid via JniUniquePtr

Java LensOverlayCoordinator owns its native
LensOverlayControllerAndroid: init() creates it and destroy() deletes
it. The ownership was a raw long plus a dedicated destroy native method,
so the type system could not tell the value apart from any other long,
and every native method accepted an arbitrary address.

Hold it as JniUniquePtr<NativeLensOverlayControllerAndroid>. Destroying
the handle deletes the native object through the deleter bound by
jni_zero::MakeUnique, so the destroy native method is removed. Native
methods take JniPtr<NativeLensOverlayControllerAndroid>, which native
receives as LensOverlayControllerAndroid*.

The null check guards destroy() exactly as the 0 check did, so a
second call is still a no-op. start() after destroy() now returns
false instead of calling native with a null pointer.

TAG=agy
CONV=d7896fff-bb31-4ee0-9555-287077259ab8

arc: Do not wait 2.5 s for mojo_proxy exit in MojoInvitationManager

MojoInvitationManager::~MojoInvitationManager() is only invoked from
ArcSessionImpl::OnStopped() after the ARC bridge has been closed. On
browser shutdown, ARCVM and external daemons (iioservice, midis)
outlive Chrome, keeping secondary portals open in mojo_proxy so its
RunLoop never quits on its own. Waiting 2.5 s in a BLOCK_SHUTDOWN task
before terminating mojo_proxy consumes almost all of session_manager's
3 s shutdown budget and causes [Shutdown hang] crashes. Reap the
process with a non-blocking check if it has already exited, and
otherwise terminate it immediately.

TAG=agy
CONV=acea79f2-f3ae-48fd-a985-33bcb9d543e4

arc: Let NearbyShareOverlayView own its child view
📋 Metadata

5a63e770b2a4c62991d374d1875c9dd6743d05a5
[ log ]

tree
3e1b601412d87c3ea9c86006a68055f0290e506a
parent
9819630ab5a4b08da28b37356bea549531c2c005 [ diff ] 
arc: Let NearbyShareOverlayView own its child view

NearbyShareSessionImpl held ProgressBarDialogView in a std::unique_ptr
while also parenting it to NearbyShareOverlayView via AddChildViewRaw().
If the exo overlay widget hosting the overlay was torn down while file
streaming was still in progress, the views hierarchy deleted the dialog
and the session's unique_ptr was left pointing at freed memory; the
progress-bar update timer and the file-handler progress callback then
dereferenced it and the eventual reset() freed it again.

Make ownership unambiguous: NearbyShareOverlayView::Show() and
ProgressBarDialogView::Show() now take a std::unique_ptr<views::View>
and hand it to AddChildView() so the views hierarchy is the single
owner. The session tracks the dialog with views::ViewTracker, which
observes the view and clears itself when the view is deleted, so the
timer / update callbacks and teardown are safe regardless of which side
destroys the overlay first.

Bug: b:501325156
Test: NearbyShareOverlayViewTest.ChildViewOwnedByOverlay
Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter="NearbyShareOverlayViewTest.*:ProgressBarDialogViewTest.*:ErrorDialogViewTest.*:LowDiskSpaceDialogViewTest.*:NearbyShareSessionImplTest.*"

[cc] Exclude PaintOpBuffer buffer lifecycle from safety checks

Wrap `PaintOpBuffer` teardown, reset, and backing buffer reallocations
in `base::ScopedSafetyChecksExclusion`:
- `~PaintOpBuffer()`: exclude `DestroyOps()` and explicitly reset
  `data_ = BufferData()` before `excluded` leaves scope so the
  `AlignedFree` of `data_` does not run unexcluded during member
  destruction.
- `Reset()`: exclude `DestroyOps()`.
- `AllocatePaintOpSlowPath()` and `ShrinkToFit()`: exclude
  `ReallocBuffer()` and `ReallocIfNeededToFit()` so the returned old
  `BufferData` temporary is freed within the exclusion scope.

Safety: these paths only destroy immutable serialized `PaintOp`
value/Skia buffers (such as `SkTextBlob`) and internal `AlignedAlloc`
backing bytes, with no JS/DOM callbacks or escaping untrusted pointers.

Speedometer3 impact: `~PaintOpBuffer` and `Reset` account for 68% of
Oilpan foreground sweeper's quarantined frees in `NewsSite-Next` and
11-14% of timed `Quarantine()` in `Perf-Dashboard`, starving
`SkTextBlob` reuse. `AllocatePaintOpSlowPath` and `ShrinkToFit` free
~500 KiB of intermediate 4-256 KiB aligned buffers per chart in
`Charts-chartjs` and drive 18% of `ThreadCache::FillBucket` misses in
`Charts-observable-plot`.

0e95ec78af9a54ea7c2462a6f98ce6455492591f

Sat Oct 24 03:22:13 2026
tree
d8c246cb9b38a0e906097dc9d6fe276b23c8f51c
parent
a290e21da28c58559947be14d20788c0d886aa01 [ diff ] 
[cc] Exclude DisplayItemList::Finalize from safety checks

During paint recording, `DisplayItemList` pre-reserves internal scratch
vectors (`visual_rects_` at 16 KiB, `offsets_` at 8 KiB, and
`paired_begin_stack_`). At the end of recording, `Finalize()` bulk-loads
`rtree_` (allocating and freeing temporary `std::vector<Branch>`
scratch) and shrinks `visual_rects_`, `offsets_`, `paired_begin_stack_`,
and `paint_op_buffer_` to fit.

All deallocations in `Finalize()` are internal POD recording scratch
vectors, `RTree::Build()` temporaries, and oversized `PaintOpBuffer`
backing buffers, with no script execution or escaping pointers.

With PartitionAlloc Scheduler-Loop Quarantine on Speedometer3, excluding
`DisplayItemList::Finalize()` eliminates:
- 52% of timed quarantine zapping in Editor-CodeMirror
- 22% in GPU Perf-Dashboard
- 11-13% in TodoMVC-JavaScript-ES6-Webpack-Complex-DOM and NewsSite-Next
- 8-16 KiB per-layer page-fault churn in TodoMVC-Angular-Complex-DOM

edcde62debce437dae6b409a32e72fbf120be31f
parent
41e024b6ec2c77d7b3352125d38740c24d80ba24 [ diff ] 
Glic: Take the browser window as JniPtr in panel state query

GlicKeyedService#isPanelShowingForBrowser() takes the native
BrowserWindowInterface as a raw long, which GlicButtonStateController
obtains from ChromeAndroidTask and glic_keyed_service_android.cc casts
back with reinterpret_cast. The long is detached from the JniUniquePtr
through which AndroidBrowserWindow owns the native window, so nothing
stops a stale value from reaching C++ as a dangling pointer.

Make isPanelShowingForBrowser() take
JniPtr<NativeBrowserWindowInterface>, obtained from
ChromeAndroidTask#getNativeBrowserWindow() added in
https://crrev.com/c/8620634, so that JNI Zero hands
BrowserWindowInterface* to C++ directly. Behavior is unchanged, except
that passing a destroyed window to native now throws
IllegalStateException in Java instead of using freed memory.

tree
330464ccfb8864862e5080885f472b581cc82085
parent
b145a8cd47bb018d86125f29e1c2341b53cd7ab0 [ diff ] 
content_shell: Close the native shell when the Activity is destroyed

ContentShellActivity.onDestroy() only detached the Java Shell view, so
the native Shell and its WebContents stayed alive until the test process
was killed, and every test leaked the WebContents it created. Closing
the last shell could not be used as a fix, because it shut down the
browser, which reaches NOTREACHED in MessagePumpAndroid::Run().

Close the active shell in onDestroy(), and do not shut down when the
last shell closes on Android, where the browser process outlives the
Activity. WebContents created by a test are now destroyed when its
Activity finishes, which lets safe JNI pointer handles held by
WebContentsImpl be released before the end-of-class LifetimeAssert
check.

TAG=agy
CONV=d7896fff-bb31-4ee0-9555-287077259ab8

tree
4cb5da8b3699a506c5e383e89ef207b349304619
parent
44a0d879139f12a582847a4cb759ce856303bfff [ diff ] 
[html-in-canvas] Disallow canvas-external SVG filters

This patch prevents elements in a canvas subtree from referencing SVG
`<filter>` elements outside the canvas subtree, similar to
https://crrev.com/c/8455562. Previously,
`FilterEffectBuilder::BuildReferenceFilter` did not check
`IsInCanvasSubtree()` on the target `SVGFilterElement`, allowing CSS
`filter`, CSS `backdrop-filter`, and SVG `filter` references to leak
external state such as `:visited` styles.

`FilterEffectBuilder` now accepts `is_in_canvas_subtree` from
`PaintLayer` and `SVGElementResourceClient`, ignoring external
`<filter>` elements when set. `SVGElement` and `SVGFilterElement` also
invalidate cached filter state in `DidChangeIsInCanvasSubtree`.
Existing `drawElementImage` filter tests that placed allowed `<filter>`
elements outside `<canvas>` are updated to define them inside
`<canvas>`.

tree

parent
a0057a50da6f5e31f9f36637e0fffa20099580c3 [ diff ] 
[WebAudio] Guard automatic pull nodes and pre-bind main-thread WeakPtrs

Previously, `DeferredTaskHandler::HasAutomaticPullNodes()` acquired
`automatic_pull_handlers_lock_` on the audio thread outside the graph
lock, but queried `automatic_pull_handlers_` (which is mutated under the
graph lock) instead of `rendering_automatic_pull_handlers_`. In
addition, `DeferredTaskHandler`, `RealtimeAudioDestinationHandler`, and
`ScriptProcessorHandler` called `weak_ptr_factory_.GetWeakPtr()` from
the audio thread when posting tasks to the main thread, even though
`WeakPtrFactory::GetWeakPtr()` lazily initializes its internal reference
owner non-atomically.

This CL annotates `automatic_pull_handlers_` with `GUARDED_BY(*this)`
and updates `HasAutomaticPullNodes()` to inspect
`rendering_automatic_pull_handlers_` under
`automatic_pull_handlers_lock_`. It also pre-binds a `weak_this_` member
on the main thread during construction of `DeferredTaskHandler`,
`RealtimeAudioDestinationHandler`, and `ScriptProcessorHandler` (matching
`AudioWorkletHandler`) so the audio thread copies an already-initialized
`WeakPtr`.

TAG=agy
CONV=2c4eb923-f4e1-4847-a380-38bc4fd0bb12

b0e95b56683e2e0f850823bb0524b1f5011293d6 [ diff ] 
[DefaultBrowser] Record default-browser state transition at startup

Chrome only notifies the user about losing default-browser status when
the change happens while it is running. A change made while Chrome is
closed is seen at the next startup as NOT_DEFAULT with no prior state,
and is never surfaced. To size that gap, classify the transition at each
startup check next to the existing DefaultBrowser.State histogram.

The previous state comes from the installer beacons
(chrome/installer/util/beacons.h), which every default-browser check
already updates: LastWasDefault and FirstNotDefault are read just before
the check mutates them. A FirstNotDefault beacon written during this
process was set by an earlier in-session check, so it implies the
previous state was default. No new persisted state is needed.

Bug: b:576390020

f3ed302b21879f9bfd34db12a6035314136032c2 [ diff ] 
[Notifications] Sequence close after DB delete

In PlatformNotificationContextImpl::DeleteNotificationData,
CloseNotifications was previously posted directly to the UI thread
before posting DoDeleteNotificationData to task_runner_. Because
WriteNotificationData first hops to task_runner_
(DoWriteNotificationData) before posting DisplayNotification back to the
UI thread, a ClosePersistentNotification call for the same persistent
notification ID (p#<origin>#1<tag>) could overtake an in-flight
WriteNotificationData or execute out of FIFO order relative to database
operations.

Move the UI-thread CloseNotifications post from DeleteNotificationData
into DoDeleteNotificationData on task_runner_ (passing
close_notification through DoDeleteNotificationData), mirroring
DoWriteNotificationData. This guarantees that DisplayNotification and
CloseNotifications execute on the UI thread in strict FIFO order with
task_runner_ database operations.

Bug: 576800304
Test: ./out/Default/content_unittests --gtest_filter="PlatformNotificationContextTest.*"

[AIM]: Add the submit energy effect to the persistent AIM button

NTP Composebox:
https://screencast.googleplex.com/cast/NTE4NzUxNTIzNjQ4MzA3MnxjM2ZhMWRmNC0xYg

Omnibox:
https://screencast.googleplex.com/cast/NDc4OTY5MTMwNjk5OTgwOHwyODRhZDZmNi0zNQ

With the energy effect enabled, pressing the persistent AI Mode button
in SEND now gives the same press feedback as cr-composebox-submit: the
pill stretches, a blurred glow slides in, and the arrow squishes. EXIT
keeps the plain button.

- The button decides in TS (`showEnergyEffect_()`: flag on and SEND).
  Only then does it mount #energy, add `.energy-effect` to the button
  and set `noink`, so no ripple shows clipped to the unstretched
  button inside the stretched pill.
- #energy sits behind the transparent button and paints the pill, the
  hover tint and the glow, so a press stretches the background but not
  the label.
- The pill widens via `inset-inline` instead of a composited scale,
  which left stale pixels on release.
- The shared motion values and the glow move into
  composebox_energy_style.css, included by both this button and
  cr-composebox-submit; only the stretch differs, 8% (UX-approved)
  instead of the submit button's 16%.
- The NTP and Omnibox composeboxes pass their existing
  energyEffectAnimationEnabled to the button.

43958cb4c6386d7c304ecee7e4d518191cdd3f24 [ diff ] 
[Dse] Use the loupe for a 3P AI Mode button until its icon loads

Currently A 3p DSE's AI Mode button showed Google's spark icon
while its own icon was being fetched, and kept it if the fetch failed.

SearchEngineService now supplies the loupe (ic_search_24dp) as soon as
a 3p DSE's AI Mode button config is available, and replaces
it with the fetched icon once it loads. If the fetch fails, the loupe
is kept. Google keeps the spark icon.

An icon fetched for a config that has since been replaced is ignored,
so that it can't replace the icon of the current search engine.

Demo: http://shortn/_vNAM7njKHA

Revert "[contextual_tasks] Allow deselecting submitted and restored tabs"

This reverts commit df6e9ee0108b12bb15e7ef8798718a77ce5931fd.

Reason for revert: Causes deterministic failure of
ContextualTasksExtensionHandlerContextLibraryBrowserTest.DeleteTabContext_HidesRestoredTabOnLaterThreadUpdates
on linux-chromeos-chrome.

Failure Link: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8667112762370905873

Original change's description:
> [contextual_tasks] Allow deselecting submitted and restored tabs
>
> The tab picker could only deselect tabs attached in the current turn.
> DeleteTabContext() only searched unsubmitted tabs, so clicking a tab
> submitted in an earlier turn, or a tab restored from the AIM thread, did
> nothing and its checkmark came back on the next update.
>
> - Submitted tabs are now deleted through DeleteFile(), which keeps
>   their request ID so the next submission reports them in
>   removed_contexts. Like DeleteFile(), this requires tab deselection
>   (ContextManagementInComposebox:enable_tab_deselection). Without it,
>   submitted tabs stay attached rather than disappearing only from the
>   UI while the server keeps using them.
> - Restored tabs have no context token, so they are hidden locally and
>   recorded as deselected. Later thread updates from AIM then keep them
>   hidden while the tab stays on the same page.
> - After a turn, AIM's thread update also restores the submitted tabs,
>   so a tab can be both. Deselecting it removes both copies, since the
>   restored copy would otherwise keep it selected.
>
> Bug: b:573759643
> Change-Id: Idc9d69c4b13dd2c72eb673eac977d36a94c6fe7d
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8576728
> Commit-Queue: James Leung <jamesleung@google.com>
> Reviewed-by: Jason Hu <hujasonx@google.com>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1726291}

Add a performance test for base::Base64Encode()

Add base/base64_perftest.cc to base_perftests to benchmark
base::Base64Encode(span<const uint8_t>) across 64 B, 1 KiB, 64 KiB, and
1 MiB inputs using Google Benchmark. Benchmark both the modp_b64 and
simdutf implementations by toggling features::kSimdutfBase64Encode.

To avoid having base::Feature code execution time show up in the
benchmark, expose the implementations as "ForTesting" functions in
base/base64.h, rather than calling base::Base64Encode() and let it
select.

This CL is AI-generated.

Output with Zen 4 on Linux:

-----------------------------------------------------
Benchmark                     Time         Throughput
-----------------------------------------------------
ModpB64/64                 76.2 ns       800.63 MiB/s
ModpB64/1024                524 ns      1863.74 MiB/s
ModpB64/65536             29619 ns      2110.28 MiB/s
ModpB64/1048576         1134726 ns       881.31 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX2/64            62.4 ns       978.94 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX2/1024           173 ns      5652.04 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX2/65536         5422 ns     11528.70 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX2/1048576     738539 ns      1354.17 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX512/64          53.2 ns      1147.02 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX512/1024        95.1 ns     10270.72 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX512/65536       2627 ns     23810.05 MiB/s
Simdutf-AVX512/1048576   704268 ns      1420.02 MiB/s

- Used SIMDUTF_FORCE_IMPLEMENTATION=haswell to force AVX2.
- For consistency, benchmarked with:
  - performance governor: echo performance > /sys/.../scaling_governor
  - Precision Boost off: echo 0 > /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpufreq/boost
  - Locked to a single core, but not core 0: taskset -c 11

Update omnibox-truncate-search-url chrome://flags choice values

Lowers the q/oq length limits in the omnibox-truncate-search-url
chrome://flags choices that truncate both parameters, so that q + oq
totals 52k instead of 60k, leaving more room in the URL for other
parameters:
- Oq30kQ30k -> Oq26kQ26k (oq 26624, q 26624)
- Oq1kQ59k  -> Oq1kQ51k  (oq 1024,  q 52224)
- Oq2kQ58k  -> Oq2kQ50k  (oq 2048,  q 51200)

The Oq1kQNoTruncation and Oq2kQNoTruncation choices are unchanged.

03898781eac5d94fb13126a851c4e56cb1b3bff9 [ diff ] 
[contextual_search] Restore ICU timezone and locale in unit tests

ComposeboxQueryControllerTest::SetUp() mutated process-global ICU
default timezone (icu::TimeZone::adoptDefault) to "America/Los_Angeles"
and locale (icu::Locale::setDefault) to "en-US" without restoring them
on teardown.

When batched in the same components_unittests shard process before
Omnibox tests that construct local times via
base::Time::FromLocalExploded (which reads libc TZ=UTC) and format them
via ICU (DocumentProviderTest.GenerateLastModifiedString and
HistoryEmbeddingsProviderTest), the leaked "America/Los_Angeles" ICU
timezone shifted formatted timestamps by 7 hours, causing ~650
flakes/day across CI/CQ.

Replace raw ICU global mutations with
base::test::ScopedRestoreDefaultTimezone and
base::test::ScopedRestoreICUDefaultLocale.

01947672a74f4078e9eedfad53a4eda7e721734e
parent
9a659a0d324918ef3967b69cc5ed7e4117d92db7 [ diff ] 
[contextual-tasks] Scope side panel thread switches to the tab

When the thread in the contextual tasks side panel changed on one tab,
e.g. from thread history in the legacy UI, all tabs affiliated with the
task moved to the new thread. Behind the `scope_side_panel_state_to_tab`
param, only the active tab now moves to the new task, and the panel
WebContents moves with it.

The other tabs keep their thread in a recreated panel WebContents, which
is created right away if one of them shows the panel, or when the panel
is reopened on one of them. The legacy UI saves the URL that last showed
the old thread when the thread changes, so that the recreated panel
loads that exact thread. The recreated panel opens the thread fresh, so
its scroll position isn't kept.

In the rearchitecture, thread history switches happen inside the AI page
without notifying the browser, so they still apply to all affiliated
tabs.

Bug: b:564556950

tree
53d5b894e52b15175a9328a0944b8c63a5b2e1d6
parent
d4c13231cc12fca9ad8eadaf164519b6ea55d1d4 [ diff ] 
[contextual-tasks] Restore and rebuild side panel on reopened tabs

When a closed tab with saved ContextualTasksTabRestoreData is reopened,
ContextualTasksSidePanelCoordinator::OnTabAdded() now rejoins the saved
task if it still exists, or creates a new task for the saved thread URL
if the task was deleted. Tabs closed with the panel closed have no
restore data and are restored without the panel.

If no other tab in the window currently has cached panel WebContents for
the task, the coordinator marks the task in
`tasks_pending_panel_rebuild_` and lazily recreates the panel
WebContents with the saved URL when the reopened tab becomes active. If
the reopened tab is closed again before activation or before its panel
finishes loading the page, GetTabRestoreData() preserves the saved task
and URL.

Bug: b:564556123
Change-Id: I3ef6124d50910b9a2c391a52a9567f2c3fc5a85a

tree
98217d52dfcc511fead6acfb1380076941d5944a
parent
dbe598353ebe102e1da47442498476e115794852 [ diff ] 
[contextual-tasks] Save side panel tab restore data on tab close

Reopening a recently closed tab with Ctrl+Shift+T or from History
restored only the page: the contextual tasks side panel and its thread
were lost.

This CL adds the `restore_side_panel_on_tab_restore` param of
ContextualTasksSidePanelTabState and saves side panel restore data when
a tab is closed with the panel open:
- Adds ContextualTasksTabRestoreData (WebContentsUserData) to store the
  saved task ID and thread URL in TabRestoreService's extra_data map
  and reattach it when a tab is restored.
- Adds ContextualTasksUiService::IsRestorableSidePanelUrl() to allow
  saving AI thread URLs and valid Search results pages (such as Lens
  searches) while excluding temporary pages like CAPTCHA or sign-in.
- Implements ContextualTasksSidePanelCoordinator::GetTabRestoreData()
  and wires it into BrowserLiveTabContext::GetExtraDataForTab() and
  chrome::CreateRestoredTab().

A follow-up CL consumes ContextualTasksTabRestoreData on tab addition to
rejoin/recreate the task and rebuild the side panel WebContents.

39033ce47fb588fe2b2746ce1081d9e382044920 [ diff ] 
[contextual-tasks] Scope side panel New thread to the tab

Clicking New thread in the contextual tasks side panel started the new
thread in the panel WebContents shared by all tabs affiliated with the
task. Behind the `scope_side_panel_state_to_tab` param, if other tabs
share the thread, the browser now moves only the active tab to a new
task with a new panel WebContents, and the other tabs keep the thread.

The browser handles the click directly in both the rearchitecture
(toolbar CreateNewThread) and the legacy UI (new
PageHandler.MoveActiveTabToNewThreadIfShared), so this doesn't depend on
detecting the zero state navigation, which
kEnableNotifyZeroStateRenderedCapability skips for cross-document
navigations.

tree
2a5a3768f9ff93d178a315a87d7ee4fb9dceff5a
parent
a96815e5e7eaa14ec440c05a9b817801048303c1 [ diff ] 
[iOS] Fix FakeSaveToPhotosSettingsMutator ABI mismatch and re-enable test

Resolve a dual-destruction crash in FakeSaveToPhotosSettingsMutator and
re-enable CanToggleAskEveryTimeAndSelectSaveToPhotosAccount.

The mutator protocol method expects a pointer to a GaiaId, but the fake
mutator declared a readwrite property with value semantics. On ARM64
Darwin, passing non-trivial C++ objects by value requires the callee to
destroy the argument before returning. Calling the protocol method
passed a pointer to a stack-allocated GaiaId; the fake setter treated
this pointer as a value parameter and destroyed the caller's stack
object. When the caller subsequently exited scope, the object was
destroyed a second time, triggering an ASan crash.

This change fixes the ABI mismatch by making the fake property readonly
and explicitly implementing the setter with a pointer parameter matching
the protocol signature.

tree
c34d886d9e9d5574d36c4af6fe8e50d74f3ece06
parent
aac9bfa87bde734337933454ff490a552bbfe8cf [ diff ] 
[UIA] Avoid repeated text traversal in Windows UIA attribute queries

CL 7801106 fixed wrapped-text annotation lookups by translating text-box
offsets into the platform ancestor's coordinates. Repeating this work
for each node can make large-range queries quadratic and block the
browser UI thread. Windows focus tracking encounters this when querying
IsReadOnly, even though that attribute does not use text offsets.

Only translate offsets when the target consumes them: AnnotationTypes
queries on text or atomic text fields. Continue querying other targets
without the unnecessary coordinate conversion.

For atomic web fields, query annotations on the relevant internal text
node rather than repeatedly scanning the entire field.

For consecutive inline text boxes under the same static-text parent,
translate the first offset and advance subsequent offsets by the
selected text length. Use the existing translation path when this
shortcut does not apply. Keep each node's query separate so mixed-value
results and selection boundaries are preserved.

During range normalization, avoid expensive endpoint comparisons when
leaf order and endpoint positions prove the range is nondegenerate. Keep
the original comparisons when that cannot be established.

fb716b6754c3ae4f40342569eab2e4f2a1b893ab
parent
f1794edd96cb2a405702a29e4a48b1daa909ec12 [ diff ] 
Add pixel tests for the WebGPU Finch kill switches

Add Pixel_WebGPUKillSwitch_* tests that set each kill switch on the
command line and check that it takes effect:

* WGSLLanguageFeature: UnsafeWGSLFeatures blocks a WGSL language
  feature. It uses the chromium_testing_* features, exposed with
  --enable-dawn-features=expose_wgsl_testing_features, so the test
  doesn't depend on which real features can currently be killed.
* WebGPUFeature: UnsafeFeatures hides WebGPU features from the adapter,
  so devices can't require them.
* DawnToggle: EnabledToggles applies a Dawn toggle. It uses
  disable_base_instance, which makes draws with a non-zero first
  instance fail validation.
* DisableWebGPU: disabling WebGPUService makes requestAdapter() return
  null.
* AdapterBlockList: blocking every adapter makes requestAdapter() return
  null.

The params are set with --enable-features=WebGPUService:<param>/<value>,
so the tests don't depend on the WebGPU study in
fieldtrial_testing_config.json. Each page draws a green square if its
checks pass and reports failure otherwise.

[PDF] Migrate password and properties dialog tests to Mocha

Migrate password and properties dialog tests from chrome.test.runTests()
to Mocha.

* Migrate the password dialog test into a single Mocha test with
  tryIncorrectPassword() and tryCorrectPassword() helpers to prevent
  order dependence.
* Remove getPasswordInput() and getSubmitButton() helper functions to
  avoid non-null assertion operators or duplicate null checks, passing
  the verified non-null dialog to helpers and inlining dialog.$.password
  and dialog.$.submit directly.
* Split the properties dialog test into per-property test cases, using
  suiteSetup() and suiteTeardown() to open and close the dialog once.

PDFExtensionPacificTimeZoneJSMochaTest doesn't run with OOPIF PDF
disabled, since the feature is default enabled and GuestView PDF has
mostly been deprecated.

Bug: 572671898

8e890cfb973ebbe22f18703035117b3fabde9cb2
parent
c0b5a7563a7721ef09e5dc03947b481264312e7b [ diff ] 
[Copy Trigger] Scan copies that Data Controls keeps in managed Chrome

When a Data Controls rule blocks copying to the OS clipboard, the data
is kept in managed Chrome and a warning message replaces it on the OS
clipboard. That path returned early and skipped copy content analysis
entirely, so a cloud DLP rule could neither block nor audit such copies.

Run content analysis after the Data Controls replace verdict and combine
the outcomes: a block or kept-in-managed-Chrome verdict from the scan
applies as before, while an allowed scan falls back to the Data Controls
replacement, which is still more restrictive than letting the data reach
the OS clipboard. Builds without content analysis keep the previous
behavior.

Bug: 325455508
Test: browser_tests --gtest_filter=*Copy_DataControlsReplacement*

8ea4f3a42e66fbd4525cf0084d9a6a633085d18d [ diff ] 
[WebNN] CoreML: Reject small stride-(2,2) float32 depthwise conv2d

When a float32 depthwise conv2d with strides == {2, 2} and fewer than 32
output elements per batch executes via CoreML E5RT, libBNNS dispatches a
specialized stride-(2, 2) AMX micro-kernel whose 16-float backward-aligned
tail store underflows before the start of the output buffer. Return
NewNotSupportedError for this configuration.

Bug: 575820393
Test: webnn_graph_impl_fuzzer --fuzz=NPU.Conv2d

c87f1901956e5539d1e92eabfe593c69227f1857 [ diff ] 
[webapps] Make Manifest nullable in ManifestManager Mojo IPCs [3/N]

Update RequestManifest() and RequestManifestDebugInfo() in
blink.mojom.ManifestManager to return a nullable Manifest? instead of a
non-nullable Manifest.

Previously, when a document could not have a manifest (e.g.,
kNoManifestAllowed on about:blank or opaque origins, or
kUnexpectedFailure when the execution context was destroyed), the
renderer sent an empty blink::mojom::Manifest::New() sentinel over Mojo.
Once blink.mojom.Manifest is typemapped to a validating blink::Manifest
class without an empty state, an empty wire struct will be rejected as a
deserialization error. Send null over Mojo for kNoManifestAllowed and
kUnexpectedFailure, report a bad message if RequestManifest() reports
kSuccess with a null manifest, and guard DevTools GotManifest(),
InstalledAppController, and PushMessagingClient against null manifests.

Bug: 485890278
Test: tools/autotest.py -C out/Default third_party/blink/renderer/modules/manifest/manifest_manager_unittest.cc third_party/blink/renderer/modules/installedapp/installed_app_controller_test.cc content/browser/manifest/manifest_browsertest.cc

Gate contextual tasks pinning on EntryPointEligibilityManager

Update ContextualTasksUIBase to use EntryPointEligibilityManager::IsPinningEligible instead of only checking the feature flag. This ensures that pinning operations and toolbar action observations are properly gated for ineligible profiles, such as Incognito.

Also updates unit and interactive UI tests to reflect the pinning eligibility check and skips pinned toolbar tests in Incognito.

3ac468f296aa1a1835417efe45da4ecb0e495b23
parent
19aeeb89270250daf0e50b363febf5b66eccd625 [ diff ] 
Fix select popup labels cut off when the popup is narrowed

Since the browser clamps popup bounds to the display work area
(crrev.com/c/8392761), a select popup whose options are wider than the
screen hides the start of every option on Windows and Linux.

list_picker.js focuses its select while the popup document is being
installed, before the popup has a size. The focus scroll finds the
select outside the empty viewport and centers it, scrolling the document
horizontally by half its width. The popup used to be exactly as wide as
its content, so the offset was clamped back to zero. Now the popup can
be narrower than its content, and the offset survives.

Focus the select with preventScroll. The select still scrolls itself to
the selected option, and the popup document never needs to scroll.

Test: InternalPopupMenuTest.NarrowedPopupIsNotScrolled (new)

Bug: 576333008

[ChroMenu] Fix infinite loop in BookmarksDynamicMenu on bookmark load

When the app menu was open and the bookmarks BuildBookmarksAction was called from the BookmarksMergedSurfaceServiceLoaded, it caused the observers to continually be added and removed from the same list. Since the list observes these changes, this led to the infinite loop, causing the browser to freeze on large bookmark lists.

be82bb8a898fe2ddc299cf92b78773dbfed15fc3
parent
fc9710fdb8de6353da2ffdbea46fe28c27db6fb9 [ diff ] 
[omnibox] Fix cut clearing the input while a match is previewed

In the "full" WebUI Omnibox popup, previewing a suggestion with
arrow-down/arrow-up, selecting part of the previewed text and cutting it
cleared the entire input row instead of cutting only the selection.

Arrow-key navigation shows the selected match in the input without
recording it in the client-side TextfieldModel, whose text stays as it
was (e.g. empty on the NTP). Since crrev.com/c/8398115, the cut handler
applies the cut to TextfieldModel and writes the model's text back to
the input. Applied to the model's stale text, the cut is a no-op, and
writing that text back replaces the previewed match, e.g. with "". The
paste handler computes the new input text from the input itself, so it
shows the right text, but it leaves the model out of sync, so a
following undo restores unrelated text.

Instead of resetting TextfieldModel to the text the input shows (which
cleared the edit history), record the switch as an edit that isn't
merged into the previous one, via a new TextfieldModel.replaceAllText().
Undo after cutting part of a previewed match now steps back through the
cut, then to the typed text, then through the typing.

Fixed: b:568495044

tree
3e89ab5176691f3232efb5d13d5d4a3b5a13995e
parent
91eefa3d342dbb6f49fdef704308f48cae59117c [ diff ] 
[Reading mode PDFs] Support nested unordered lists

Track a stack of list levels, each with its marker indentation and last
list item.

A marker indented deeper than the innermost level opens a nested `kList`
inside the previous `kListItem`, matching the accessibility tree
produced by Blink, and an outdent returns to the matching ancestor
level.

A marker more than 4 em (of the item text's font size) to the right
starts a new list instead, since it is likely in another column. Parent
list items' bounds now include their nested sublists.

Bug: 525509231

6b5bee35b59d91c2c77df8981c245069f8ce4cbe [ diff ] 
Modernize containers and improve efficiency in local_search_service

Follow up https://crrev.com/c/8593801 with additional optimization
opportunities across LinearMapSearch, InvertedIndexSearch, and
InvertedIndex:

- Replace std::map, std::unordered_map, and std::unordered_set with
  absl::flat_hash_map and absl::flat_hash_set, and eliminate redundant
  map lookups.
- Add an ID tie-breaker to CompareResults() so equal-scoring search
  results sort deterministically when iterating over unordered hash
  maps.
- Declare defaulted move constructors and move assignment operators on
  shared structs, and use move semantics, emplace_back, and base::Extend
  to avoid unnecessary copies.
- Use std::u16string_view keys and pre-reserve vector and hash table
  capacities to reduce allocations and rehashing.

Benchmarked in release mode, this speeds up LinearMapSearch::AddOrUpdate
by ~37-39% (saving ~0.48-0.62 ms per Ash browser_tests startup, or
~7.0-9.0 s of CPU time across the suite) and the end-to-end
InvertedIndexSearch pipeline on a 500-document Help App batch by ~47-49%
(saving ~35-47 ms per pass), for a total savings of ~8.4-12.8 s of CPU
time (~2.1-3.2 s of cumulative shard wall time) across browser_tests.

[protobuf] Add roll_protobuf.py continue/drop/resume CLI and README docs

The `ContinueRoll`, `DropCurrentPatch`, and `ResumeRoll` helpers are in
place, but `roll_protobuf.py` does not yet expose them as CLI
subcommands or document the conflict-resolution workflow in
`README.chromium`.

Wire the `continue [--allow-extra-files]`, `drop --reason`, and `resume`
subcommands into `main()` (returning exit code 2 when a subsequent patch
conflicts and 0 when replay completes), update `FormatRollStateReport`
conflict hints, and add the "When a patch conflicts" section to
`//third_party/protobuf/README.chromium`.

Verified via `RollProtobufStartTest` end-to-end CLI tests for
`continue`, `drop`, and `resume`, plus `PRESUBMIT_test.py` and
`git cl presubmit`.

837a3f563ff7de5f636250ef3e221eaf1311bac8 [ diff ] 
[protobuf] Add ResumeRoll to take back control after manual roll commits

When a roller resolves or drops one or more patches by hand using the
git commands in `README.chromium`, `GITDIR/protobuf-roll/state.json`
falls behind the branch's `HEAD`.

Add `ResumeRoll` to verify that the working tree is clean and `HEAD`
descends from `start_commit`, inspect the commit subjects in
`start_commit..HEAD` (`"Import upstream protobuf ..."`,
`"Apply patches/NNNN-name.patch"`, and
`"Drop patches/NNNN-name.patch (...)"`), reconcile `RollState`
(`expected_head`, `current_index`, and `patch_results`), and continue
replaying the remaining patches in the chain.

Verified via `RollProtobufStartTest` covering resuming after manual
`Apply` and `Drop` commits as well as refusal on dirty working trees or
unexpected commit subjects.

[protobuf] Add DropCurrentPatch to drop conflicting roll patches

During a protobuf roll, a conflicting patch may turn out to be obsolete
or superseded upstream and need to be dropped cleanly mid-replay.

Add `PatchReplayStatus.DROPPED`, `_CleanUntrackedAndBackupFiles` (shared
with `AbortRoll` so git-ignored `.orig`/`.rej` files are removed before
`git clean -fdq`), `_RejectStagedChangesOutsideProtobuf` (shared with
`ContinueRoll`), and `DropCurrentPatch(reason, ...)` to validate a
single-line non-empty reason, an active conflict state, and no staged
changes outside `//third_party/protobuf`, discard the conflicting
patch's working-tree and index changes under `//third_party/protobuf`,
`git rm` its `patches/NNNN-name.patch` file, commit
`"Drop patches/NNNN-name.patch (<reason>)"`, and continue replaying the
remaining patches.

Verified via `RollProtobufStartTest` covering invalid-reason and
outside-staged rejection, `.orig`/`.rej` and untracked-directory
cleanup, patch deletion, commit message formatting, and continuation
across subsequent patches.

[DomStorage] Avoid redundant SQLite update work

Skip preparing cached deletion and insertion statements when a map
update has no corresponding keys or entries. Move entry buffers into
binding and compression rather than copying them through const
references.

Remove the extra metadata DELETE executed after the deletion loop, when
the statement's storage-key binding has already been cleared.

Add IPH toast for Contextual Tasks pinned icon change

Adds a one-time toast in-product help (IPH) promo informing users who previously pinned the Contextual Tasks toolbar shortcut that its icon has been updated to the Super G icon.

The promo is requested on startup for eligible users who already had the shortcut pinned prior to entering the Super G icon arm, and is closed or aborted if the user clicks the shortcut or unpins it. This CL also registers the IPH feature, strings, metrics, and associated interactive UI tests.

[a11y] Add HoverTextEventHandler skeleton and controller plumbing

Adds the HoverTextEventHandler skeleton in Ash and wires hover point
and key event forwarding through AccessibilityController and
AccessibilityControllerClient when Hover Text is enabled.

HoverTextEventHandler registers as a pre-target accessibility event
handler and tracks whether Hover Text is active via Search key hold or
the shelf status area tray toggle. Event handling business logic and
HoverTextEventHandler unit tests follow in the next change in the
stack.

d23ec229fd362c69781ce1ff5b776faaf86cd267
parent
0b7fe8ceff75ae5230776c3b0f90724c023152c5 [ diff ] 
[GEiC] Read the GEiC configuration from the Glic provider latch

Unify GEiC configuration latching with standard Glic Provider Latch.

This Change migrates Gemini Enterprise in Chrome (GEiC) from its bespoke
lazy latching mechanism (which stored config on `base::SupportsUserData`
on first read) to the standard, eager `GlicProviderLatch` system used by
standard Glic.

Lazy resolution created race conditions and dependencies on caller
ordering and policy load timing. Eager latching resolves the provider
and configuration at keyed service creation, ensuring consistent state
throughout the profile lifetime and blocking late policy applications
from leaving the session in an inconsistent state.

* **Decouple GEiC Enablement and Config reads**: Removes `geic::IsGeicEnabled()` and `geic::GetGeicGuestUrl()` in favor of direct queries to `GlicProviderLatch` and `GlicProviderSpec`.
* **Centralize Guest URL Logic**: Transfers `MaybeAppendAuthUser` and localized URL synthesis into `GlicProviderSpec::GetGuestUrl()`, isolating provider-specific URL nuances within the specs themselves.
* **Streamline Configuration Verification**: Unconditionally parses the GEiC configuration once at startup, keeping subsequent evaluation deterministic and lightweight.

Bug: 569015617, 574499029, 574535435

5e77cf58d412006ad5db9171f302c048f25cfa26 [ diff ] 
Apply contoured border outsets after pixel snapping

PixelSnappedContouredBorderWithOutsets expanded the border rect in
LayoutUnit space, so a huge outline-offset saturated the size and
folded the far edge back into the viewport. Snap the border rect first,
then apply integer gfx::Outsets to avoid LayoutUnit size saturation.
Round fractional outsets at call sites and remove the obsolete
zero-size snap helper. With zero outsets, the result now matches
PixelSnappedContouredBorder.

[contextual_tasks] Restore context library tabs before task ID is set

post message can arrive before mtid is reflected in the tab URL and a
task ID is created. The function that handled this post message
HandleThreadContextLibraryUpdateFromAim() returned early when there was
no task id (GetEffectiveTaskId() was nullopt). This incorrectly caused a
bug: dropping the restored tabs.

Fix:
- Update the session handle's restored tabs, clear submitted context
  tokens, and update the context library input state even when
  task id is null (GetEffectiveTaskId() is nullopt), only preventing
  ContextualTasksService::SetUrlResourcesFromServer() from running
  in that case.
- Update tests.

[remoting] Position DisconnectWindowMac on the primary screen

`[DisconnectWindowController setDialogPosition]` previously used
`NSScreen.mainScreen`, which returns the display containing the window
with keyboard focus rather than the system's primary display. On
multi-monitor setups, this caused the disconnect window to appear on or
jump to whichever display had focus when shown.

Use `NSScreen.screens.firstObject` instead so the disconnect window is
consistently anchored on the primary display, matching the behavior on
Windows and Linux.

815c5675405c7aafaa71c88c8d3c77d95a31fb42
parent
ea09c6bf368b7a75b7f4b9acd876911edea2336b [ diff ] 
[DevTools] Handle unloadable last used profile in Target domain

`ProfileManager::GetLastUsedProfile()` returns `nullptr` if the last
used profile can't be loaded, for example because it isn't a direct
child of the user data directory, its directory can't be created, or
it's scheduled for deletion. Chrome's own UI falls back to the profile
picker in that case, but the DevTools code assumed that the default
profile is always available:

- `Target.createTarget` without a `browserContextId` asserted that the
  profile exists (a `CHECK` until https://crrev.com/c/8618683 turned
  it back into a `DCHECK`), and passed `nullptr` to `Navigate()`
  otherwise.
- `Target.createBrowserContext` and every `browserContextId` lookup
  (for example in `Target.createTarget` or `Browser.setPermission`)
  dereferenced the profile unconditionally.
- `/json/new` passed the profile to `Navigate()` without a null check.

Report errors instead, consistent with how unknown browser contexts are
already handled, and add a regression test that makes the last used
profile unloadable.

TAG=agy
CONV=c11b3e11-7b8c-45f3-939b-650ff2df0064

[DCSI] Add diagnostic UMA histograms to CompoundImageBacking

Add two patterned sparse histograms split by {CopyType} (Static vs.
Dynamic) to separate static shared-memory-to-GPU uploads from dynamic
allocation copies and to provide long-term observability for debugging
any dynamic backing allocations and copies across backings:

- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.ContentSync.{CopyType}.CopyInfo:
Encodes the exact (source backing, destination backing, triggering access stream) tuple as `src_type * 10000 + dst_type * 100 + stream` (SSDDAA).

- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.ContentSync.{CopyType}.InitialSharedImageUsage:
Records the initial 30-bit SharedImageUsage bitmask separately for
Static and Dynamic copies, replacing the unsplit
GPU.CompoundImageBacking.ContentSync.InitialSharedImageUsage.

Also add supplementary DynamicAllocation histograms:
- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.DynamicAllocation.IncompatibleExistingBackingType
- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.DynamicAllocation.HasSharedMemoryBacking
- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.DynamicAllocation.IsTransientBacking
- GPU.CompoundImageBacking.DynamicAllocation.Success

Finally, add the missing DawnImageBacking entry to
SharedImageBackingType in
tools/metrics/histograms/metadata/gpu/enums.xml and unit test coverage
in CompoundImageBackingCopyToGpuMemoryBufferTest.

GPU.CompoundImageBacking.ContentSync.InitialSharedImageUsage replaced
by

Fix flake in SuccessLogsRedactedCommandLine native messaging test

SuccessLogsRedactedCommandLine invokes
StartupBrowserCreator::ProcessCommandLineAlreadyRunning(), which posts a
task to the IO thread and then to a ThreadPool background task
(LaunchContext::LaunchInBackground) that logs the native messaging host
command line via VLOG(1).

However, the test called log.StopCapturingLogs() immediately after
ProcessCommandLineAlreadyRunning() returned on the UI thread, before
waiting for catcher.GetNextResult(). When the background task ran after
StopCapturingLogs(), the expected VLOG(1) message was missed.

Wait for catcher.GetNextResult() before calling log.StopCapturingLogs().

Test history:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/%3A%2F%2Fchrome%2Ftest%5C%3Abrowser_tests%21gtest%3A%3ANativeMessagingLaunchLoggingApiTest%23SuccessLogsRedactedCommandLine

tree
f169f25771e340d3db0ddce5a338781824cc3c15
parent
9600796bc5d1c9b5cf8dfd152539976f9064e886 [ diff ] 
[canvas] Prevent reentrant context replacement in getContext

During canvas rendering context creation, synchronous events dispatched
upon initialization failure could reentrantly execute script and attach
a new context before the outer factory call completed. When the outer
call finished, assigning its return value directly to the host's context
pointer could overwrite or detach the existing context, leading to
orphaned contexts with stale host associations.

Ensure that the created context is assigned to a local variable first,
and verify that no context was bound and the canvas host remains in a
valid state before updating the host's context reference. If the host
was already bound during the creation call, dispose of the newly created
context. In addition, ensure CanvasRenderingContext::Dispose only
detaches from the host if it is the currently active context.

Initial patch and tests generated by CodeMender.

SuperSize: Memoize archive._NormalizeNames() results

Symbol names repeat heavily both within a .size file and across the
.size / .sizediff files that are loaded together (e.g. by
create_patched_supersize_diff.py). Cache normalized names per
(section_name, full_name) in a module-level dict so repeated names
skip parsing.

Expected speed-up: ~8.5s on create_patched_supersize_diff.py
(_NormalizeNames over all 4 symbol lists: 16.49s -> 7.97s, with
identical output).

Soong's default header globs via extra_include_dir_deps:

1. Add extra_include_dir_deps support to CcModule in soong_ast.py and
   rebase it in gen_android_bp.py (_rebase_module).
2. Add a ${GN2BP_MODULE_PREFIX}libcxx_headers filegroup in
   Android.extras.bp.template covering bundled libc++ extensionless
   headers and runtime/*.ipp files, and reference it from
   ${GN2BP_MODULE_PREFIX}cc_defaults.
3. Add third_party/googletest/src/googletest/src/gtest-port.cc to
   extra_include_dir_deps for //third_party/googletest:gtest (textually
   #included by gtest_port_wrapper.cc).
4. Add src/**/*.h and src/**/*.inc to extra_include_dir_deps in
   boringssl_Android.bp.template (since BoringSSL sources #include
   internal headers and .inc files under src/crypto/ while
   local_include_dirs only declares src/include).

tree
d8adbcfa42b2eaec62ecd4891abb684cb7e61ac7
parent
a84a75c8a71a15642c516283dd6343088c8ea580 [ diff ] 
[Unified Tabstrip] Fix resize when switching active split tab state

GetChildCrossoverWidth() previously handled TabView and TabGroupView,
but fell through to GetMinimumSize() for SplitTabView. Because
SplitTabView::GetMinimumSize() sums its children's current active or
inactive minimum widths, SplitTabView's crossover width changed based on
whether it contained the active tab. Above crossover width, switching
the active tab between a SplitTabView and a regular TabView shifted
total_crossover_width and caused all tabs in the strip (both ungrouped
and inside expanded TabGroupViews) to resize.

Compute SplitTabView's crossover width by summing the crossover widths
of its direct children minus tab overlap so above-crossover proportional
tab widths remain invariant across active-tab changes.

[WebApps] Add debug value rule to webapps-testing-review skill

Add WEBAPPS_ASSERT_OUTCOMES_NOT_COMMAND_DEBUG_VALUES to the web apps
testing review skill. Tests must assert command results, app state, or
histograms, not the keys of command debug values. Debug values exist for
chrome://web-app-internals and are not a contract. A reviewer asked for
this rule in crrev.com/c/8615707.

Addresses several issues in remoting/test/frame_generator:
- Fix globalAlpha leak in high_motion.html so background clears are
  opaque across frames.
- Increase velocity in chaos_stress.html to match its high-speed motion
  intent.
- Pass ?benchmark=1 from frame_generator.py and skip auto-starting the
  live requestAnimationFrame loop in benchmark mode so frame 0 cannot
  race with wall-clock animation frames.
- Compare row spans for partial right-edge blocks in
  DifferFrameGenerator instead of unconditionally marking them changed
  when frame width is not a multiple of kBlockSize.
- Fall back to a system Chrome/Chromium binary on PATH in
  frame_generator.py when out/Release/chrome is not present.

tree
a745de4731c922438dc2cc6c70c75b1b067aaa19
parent
1e246656b620831ca6b4d28093201bb53aed0077 [ diff ] 
[Reading mode PDFs] Extract ruling lines from line segments

Add `pdf::ExtractRulingLines()`, which turns the raw line segments from
`PDFiumPage` into horizontal and vertical ruling lines for the heuristic
tree builder:
- Keep segments within 1.5 px of horizontal or vertical and at least
  8 px long, snapped to their centerline.
- Merge lines that sit on the same straight line, allowing up to 2 px of
  misalignment, when they touch or overlap. This joins adjacent cell
  borders and lines drawn in several pieces into continuous lines.

This is a pre-requisite for labeling tables.

6480b9c9e6c51624e228ae4ae1438c8512ecdc4c [ diff ] 
[Masonry] Add tests for reading-flow across tracks

This creates tests for reading-flow across tracks, using the suggested
"reading row" implementation from the CSSWG issue [1] discussing reading
flow across tracks. This covers dense-packing, track-reverse, fill-
reverse, and writing direction impact on reading-flow across tracks.

In this change, we're counting an item as part of the reading row if at
least 2/3 of an item fit into the reading row's cutoff.

[1]
https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/5675#issuecomment-4848519759

[DomStorage] Wait for writes in DOMStorageBrowserTest delete key

The test page signals completion after updating its local cache, but
writes reach the storage service asynchronously. Querying usage
immediately can therefore report no stored data.

Wait for exactly one storage key with nonzero usage before deleting it.
Checking the size also avoids treating an area that is still loading as
a completed write.

parent
667f9de01e7b3befd64cbe2e7630ca768b270c4a [ diff ] 
[contextual_tasks] Remove post message to avoid late listeners

When context_library.html or tab_picker.html loads,
post_message_entrypoint.js may call
ExtensionBrowserProxyImpl.getInstance() and bind
CreateExtensionPageHandler (the mojo pipe) before listeners like
context_library.js or tab_picker.js attach.

This is because the mojo pipe setup is different from the listeners due
to extension iframes having one main non-lit html that imports the lit
component as well as the post message handler.

If the post message pipe is attached first, and a tab is sent to the
listeners in context library, but the listener is not initialized yet,
this tab update is lost.

One fix would be to cache the latest tab state to send once the listener
attaches.

However, post_message_entrypoint.js is no longer needed now that AIM
communication uses the extension-wide service worker instead of
per-iframe postMessage.

Fix: Delete the post message entrypoint since it is causing race
conditions and not needed anymore. This will allow for no more dropped
tab messages since the listener will be registered on time for the same
pipe, given they are all part of the same component now (context
library).

Additional fixes: Remove post message handler from rest of components.

[contextual_tasks] Mount context library for persisted/restored tabs

The context library was only mounted while there were unsubmitted tabs.
So it never appeared when historical tabs (submitted in past sessions in
the same thread) existed.

- Add HasContextLibraryTabs(), which checks if submitted (persisted)
  tabs and restored tabs with a tab ID exist.
- Ensure that inject input is sent when restored tabs exist too, not
  just attached tabs (given `HasContextLibraryTabs()`)
- Update the context library input state after AIM sends the thread's
  restored tabs, since they may be the only tabs in the session.

parent
60677fd11ab3a55f66670cc2ee091879c65b9d89 [ diff ] 
[Actor] Route PiP actions through delegate to fix missing pause button

Currently, the PiP pause/play actions for ActorTask disappear because
they are applied directly to the activity and instantly overwritten.
The Jetpack PiP delegate commits its own internal state on top of the
activity, wiping out any actions that were not registered with it.

Because the delegate keeps its params across commits, setActions must
run on every update (including when no task is active) and on destroy()
to prevent stale buttons from remaining visible. This routes the
RemoteAction list through BasicPictureInPicture instead, calling
setActions on mPipDelegate unconditionally on every update, ensuring the
pause and resume buttons are correctly preserved or hidden.

fc39f4d5d642f028f3376e24edca69985beb56cd
parent
f56285c2feb48d79fa7729991cc6f70984718b3f [ diff ] 
[Composebox] Add kDynamicAimFulfillment main switch and settings

kDynamicAimFulfillmentRealbox and kDynamicAimFulfillmentOmnibox pick
the surfaces for dynamic AIM fulfillment: composebox queries go to the
Search results page instead of AI Mode, unless the active tool, model or
attached inputs require AI Mode. This adds omnibox::kDynamicAimFulfillment
(disabled by default) as the main switch: a surface uses dynamic AIM
fulfillment only if kDynamicAimFulfillment and that surface's feature
are both enabled. With kDynamicAimFulfillment disabled, every
composebox query routes to AI Mode as today.

kDynamicAimFulfillment also holds the settings both surfaces share:
- srp_input_phase (default "single_image"): which attached inputs may
  go to the Search results page ("none", "single_image", "multi_image"
  or "any_combination").
- use_server_input_rules (default false): use the server-provided phase
  when present.

The settings are shared rather than per surface because the routing
logic that reads them lives in InputStateModel, which is shared by both
surfaces and doesn't know which one it serves.

Adds IsDynamicAimFulfillmentEnabledForRealbox() and
IsDynamicAimFulfillmentEnabledForOmnibox(), which check both features,
and uses them for the dynamicAimFulfillmentEnabled load-time data in
NewTabPageUI and OmniboxPopupUI so the page's styling matches routing.

Everything lives in composebox_features next to the per-surface
features; InputStateModel (components/contextual_search) already
depends on that target. Follow-up CLs add the routing logic.

14a0b222a2bb876b1f158cd1f76eb64371e6f733
parent
5e95cbdb49bef97f291f6e5bf7bf60ba42a29456 [ diff ] 
[BottomControls] Fix clipped composited screenshot during resize

During bottom bar height transitions (such as switching between 48dp and
56dp), the composited layer could retain a clipped screenshot. The
resource adapter allowed captures to run while a layout pass was
pending, capturing pre-layout dimensions and consuming the snapshot
token. When layout subsequently executed and resized the view, recapture
was locked out because the token was already marked as captured. In
addition, the view binder mutated the container slot height in-place
without scheduling layout.

Invalidating the token and requesting a capture on size changes in
onLayoutChange() and setting layout params on slot height changes
guarantees the compositor recaptures an unclipped screenshot matching
the final view bounds.

Before:
https://drive.google.com/file/d/13ZfyDz4kZ04_lYGARJE00Fxv-uYEatv0/view?usp=drivesdk
After:
https://drive.google.com/file/d/1ltIP3OUaprBs02WGjyVSSpBTZKoBA2Z1/view?usp=drivesdk
Side-by-side:
https://drive.google.com/file/d/1CmbG4m9FE8LVCdRdi7ly9bGAGiAZEjDW/view?usp=drivesdk

2dd9c134541a64cf8ba7e643847c43f7d7bfb910

083ce9f2ea49fa5280fed3a87ff7c42063229567 [ diff ] 
Speculative load measurement - move to dictionaries

The current implementation of `performance.getSpeculations()` returns an
object with no toJSON implementation. That means it doesn’t serialize
its data.

Moving the return values to dictionaries seems more idiomatic
from a WebIDL perspective, and enables serialization with less code.

tree
154e04ff67169f706336e6726540e7c1f6953616
parent
179d1113e9fe12f1589b4fffc16442d4740d627d [ diff ] 
[GPU] Wait for focus in WebGL fullscreen test

In ContextLost_WebGLContextNotLostFromFullscreenChange,
Page.bringToFront is sent over DevTools immediately before clicking
#enterFullscreenButton. On macOS, window activation after
Page.bringToFront is asynchronous via WindowServer IPC, so
RenderWidgetHostView::HasFocus() can still be false when
requestFullscreen() executes ~40ms later, causing
RenderFrameHostImpl::EnterFullscreen() to reject the request under
kSuppressFullscreenFromUnfocusedView.

Wait for document.hasFocus() after Page.bringToFront before clicking the
fullscreen button, and log errors in webgl_with_fullscreen.html on
failure to aid future diagnostics.

[Unified Tabstrip] Avoid clipping horizontal split tabs on unsplit animation

Fri Oct 23 22:00:01 2026
tree
3077b19209ab8df954d78d7604ae31b6f47ebec5
parent
e5b5909e16fc7c1ca8d402d3ccc60fab7bcc5f02 [ diff ] 
[Unified Tabstrip] Avoid clipping horizontal split tabs on unsplit animation

When a split tab is unsplit, TabCollectionNode::MoveChild calls
TabCollectionAnimatingLayoutManager::AnimateAndReparentView, which
creates a ui::Layer on the remaining TabView and reparents it.

Previously, TabView::UpdateParentLayer called
UpdateLayerRoundedCorners unconditionally, applying
kVerticalTabCornerRadius + GetInsets().height() to the layer even in
horizontal orientation and clipping the horizontal tab's
outward-flaring bottom corners until the layer was destroyed at
animation end. In addition, UpdateParentLayers runs before
AddedToWidget -> OnTabStateChanged, so vertical pinned tabs reparented
out of a split kept the split inset inflation on their layer corner
radius and clip path.

Guard UpdateLayerRoundedCorners with TabStripOrientation::kVertical in
UpdateParentLayer, and refresh the vertical clip path and layer
rounded corners via UpdateClipPathAndRoundedCorners in AddedToWidget.

tree
14d1d2023c891a3e8d32ab4263e1c44ee3e325ab
parent
fe1e876f264007ae92acef08f70ceed118806d4e [ diff ] 
[policy] Fix UserPolicySigninService observer ordering and recovery

Ensure UserPolicySigninService initializes and recovers managed user
policies.

UserPolicySigninServiceFactory lacked a dependency on
SigninProfileAttributesUpdaterFactory, causing
UserPolicySigninService::OnPrimaryAccountChanged to evaluate policy
eligibility before ProfileAttributesEntry was populated with account
credentials. Additionally, mobile ShutdownCloudPolicyManager wiped
policy on startup without resetting the policy check timestamp, which
caused subsequent management acceptance to delay registration by 1 to 3
days instead of recovering immediately.

UserPolicySigninServiceFactory now declares
DependsOn(SigninProfileAttributesUpdaterFactory::GetInstance()),
guaranteeing profile attributes are updated before sign-in events are
handled.
UserPolicySigninServiceMobile::OnProfileUserManagementAcceptanceChanged
validates the profile path and clears policy_prefs::kLastPolicyCheckTime
to trigger immediate registration. Furthermore,
policy_prefs::kLastPolicyCheckTime is cleared during
ShutdownCloudPolicyManager and cleared in GetTryRegistrationDelay when
the underlying policy store reports an error.

UserPolicySigninServiceTest.UpdaterRunsBeforePolicyServiceOnSignIn fails
without the fix and passes with it. Reverting the fix reproduces the
failure, and 20 repeat runs and 19 related unit tests pass.

The tests and the fix were written with AI assistance.

1b298e0f636fcf505b424ca8500e0449487bd04d
parent
4994b35d0e95a034a1f8c26972c4046c24ea33ae [ diff ] 
[Glic] Optimize browser tests (faster WaitForGuest)

Glic tests are some of the most time-consuming tests in
android_browsertests, using up to 15 minutes of bot time total.

GlicApiBrowserTestMixin::WaitForGuest polled for the guest frame with an
exponential backoff that started at 200 ms and doubled up to 2 s. The
guest typically becomes ready ~1 s after the panel opens, so the loop
was usually asleep in its 800 ms slot at that moment and every Glic API
test idled for ~0.5 s before noticing.

Poll every 25 ms initially and grow by 1.25x up to the 2 s cap (instead
of doubling from 200 ms), so the interval stays small during normal ~1 s
startup while still backing off to 2 s on slow bots. Earlier versions of
this change also added a WebContentsObserver to wake the loop on guest
load events, but it did not save anything measurable. This version is
simpler.

Measured with the 25-50 ms poll on a local x86_64 Android 15 emulator
with android-x64-rel GN args, medians of 3 runs per test, native test
duration:
- testDefaultInvocationSource: 2625 ms -> 2143 ms (-18%)
- testRecordUseCounter: 2587 ms -> 2345 ms (-9%)
- testAdditionalContext: 2974 ms -> 2189 ms (-26%)
- testGetFocusedTabStateV2WithNavigation: 3039 ms -> 2451 ms (-19%)
- testSwitchConversationToExistingInstance: 4640 ms -> 3560 ms (-23%)
The time between the guest defining runApiTest and the fixture noticing
drops from 480-610 ms to 0-4 ms. Over 8 runs each of 3 tests, this
version and the observer version were indistinguishable (pooled medians
2146 / 2112 ms).

On an android-x64-rel CQ dry run (25 shards), paired against a main run
from the same day: summed GTest time across 170 GlicApi* cases drops
1048 s -> 977 s (-6.8%; median 5.93 s -> 5.54 s), across the 96
GlicApiTest cases 648 s -> 597 s (-7.9%; median 6.43 s -> 5.82 s), and
across all 603 *Glic* cases 2442 s -> 2367 s (-3.1%). The 1527 non-Glic
cases are flat (+1.1%, within bot noise).

Bug: 576702252, 573949413
Test: out/AndroidPhoneRel/bin/run_android_browsertests --gtest_filter=GlicApiTest.testDefaultInvocationSource/Default:GlicApiTest.testRecordUseCounter/Default:GlicApiTest.testAdditionalContext/Default:GlicApiTest.testSwitchConversationToExistingInstance/Default:GlicApiTest.testGetFocusedTabStateV2WithNavigation/Default

2026-10-22 lyjiang Replace the global absl::Mutex in webgpu/instance.cc
  with a function-local static pointer (GetFlushMutex()) matching
  GetFlushMap(). On macOS and non-NDEBUG builds, absl::Mutex::~Mutex()
  is not inline-empty, so a global absl::Mutex registers an exit-time
  destructor via __cxa_atexit and adds a static initializer when
  statically linked into Chromium.
